# Parsnips

Parsnips in the HMI library has 70 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, Ca, K, Mg and P from 5 sources.<sup <li>
<strong>Metals measured:</strong>

Lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury and nickel and others, in 70 results from 5 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Norton GJ, Deacon CM, Mestrot A, Feldmann J, Jenkins P, Baskaran C, Meharg AA · 2015</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Cadmium and lead in vegetable and fruit produce selected from specific regional areas of the UK</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">The Science of the Total Environment 533, 520-527 (2015); accepted author manuscript</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/nikolic2014-novi-sad-market-vegetables-metals|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Nikolić NP, Borišev MK, Pajević SP, Arsenov DD, Župunski MD · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/nikolic2014-novi-sad-market-vegetables-metals">Comparative assessment of mineral elements and heavy metals accumulation in vegetable species</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food and Feed Research 41(2): 115-123, 2014</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/nikolic2014-novi-sad-market-vegetables-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Joint FAO/WHO Expert Committee on Food Additives · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022">Cadmium: dietary exposure assessment</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">WHO Food Additives Series, No. 82 (Safety evaluation of certain contaminants in food, prepared by the 91st meeting of JECFA)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Main drivers:</strong>

Form, basis and where the parsnips was sampled; reporting limits decide how many results are non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Norton GJ, Deacon CM, Mestrot A, Feldmann J, Jenkins P, Baskaran C, Meharg AA · 2015</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Cadmium and lead in vegetable and fruit produce selected from specific regional areas of the UK</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">The Science of the Total Environment 533, 520-527 (2015); accepted author manuscript</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/nikolic2014-novi-sad-market-vegetables-metals|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Nikolić NP, Borišev MK, Pajević SP, Arsenov DD, Župunski MD · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/nikolic2014-novi-sad-market-vegetables-metals">Comparative assessment of mineral elements and heavy metals accumulation in vegetable species</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food and Feed Research 41(2): 115-123, 2014</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/nikolic2014-novi-sad-market-vegetables-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Joint FAO/WHO Expert Committee on Food Additives · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022">Cadmium: dietary exposure assessment</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">WHO Food Additives Series, No. 82 (Safety evaluation of certain contaminants in food, prepared by the 91st meeting of JECFA)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Practical framing:</strong>

Ask for lot results with a quantification limit well below the limit that applies in the destination market.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Norton GJ, Deacon CM, Mestrot A, Feldmann J, Jenkins P, Baskaran C, Meharg AA · 2015</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Cadmium and lead in vegetable and fruit produce selected from specific regional areas of the UK</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">The Science of the Total Environment 533, 520-527 (2015); reported lead in parsnips, as sold from 322 results in 2 reporting regions, with subgroup medians from 5.35 to 50 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Norton et al. 2015 reported lead at 11.3 µg/kg (median) and cadmium at 7.1 µg/kg (median) in parsnips, as sold from SW Britain (Devon and Cornwall) basket survey.<sup class="cite">[[sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Norton GJ, Deacon CM, Mestrot A, Feldmann J, Jenkins P, Baskaran C, Meharg AA · 2015</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Cadmium and lead in vegetable and fruit produce selected from specific regional areas of the UK</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">The Science of the Total Environment 533, 520-527 (2015); accepted author manuscript</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) 2014 reported inorganic arsenic at 9.2 µg/kg (mean) in parsnips, as sold from European dietary-survey occurrence data.<sup class="cite">[[sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Dietary exposure to inorganic arsenic in the European population</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">EFSA Journal 2014;12(3):3597</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the parsnips came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.

Results stay on the basis each source reports: as sold or fresh weight, dry weight, or unstated. Dry-weight, as-sold and unstated results are never pooled, and no moisture conversion is applied.

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-parsnips" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-parsnips-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of parsnips, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

No matched reference or pooled distribution is inferred.</p></div></details><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-baseline-fallback id="hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:parsnips-d71b366f659d:d32cc6c77fa2"><summary>HMI parsnips Pb reference · Parsnips, as sold</summary><div data-baseline-reference-template="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:parsnips-d71b366f659d:d32cc6c77fa2"><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Reference and scope</p><p>HMI parsnips Pb reference · Parsnips, as sold. Parsnips, as sold; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>11.3</td><td>11.3</td><td>15.5–26 (bounded)</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>11.3</td><td>11.3</td><td>14–25 (bounded)</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>35 reference units; 2 sampling families. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>34</td><td>50%</td></tr><tr><td>Norton et al. 2015</td><td>1</td><td>50%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for parsnips, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>2 sampling families with different designs; none is a probability sample of world supply. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li><li><a href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead" class="internal" data-slug="sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead" data-no-cite-meta>Norton et al. 2015</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Method: hmi-pooled-reference-v2.</p><p>hmi-pooled-reference-v2:parsnips-d71b366f659d:d32cc6c77fa2</p><p>The reference is computed by HMI from admitted evidence, not copied from a study baseline. The HMI reference for lead in parsnips, as sold pools 35 subgroup units from 2 sampling families, with a median of 11.3 µg/kg and a middle half spanning 11.3 (lower quartile) and between 15.5 and 26 µg/kg (upper quartile).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Norton GJ, Deacon CM, Mestrot A, Feldmann J, Jenkins P, Baskaran C, Meharg AA · 2015</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Cadmium and lead in vegetable and fruit produce selected from specific regional areas of the UK</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">The Science of the Total Environment 533, 520-527 (2015); Targeted parsnips, as sold samples in EURO had cadmium higher at 31 µg/kg, against 20 µg/kg for EURO in the same study.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

In one study, lead across 6 groups of parsnips ranged from 20 µg/kg (locality I (green markets, Novi Sad)) to 6,290 µg/kg (locality V (green markets, Novi Sad)); the lowest is the lowest observed in that study, not an established sourcing preference.<sup class="cite">[[sources/nikolic2014-novi-sad-market-vegetables-metals|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Nikolić NP, Borišev MK, Pajević SP, Arsenov DD, Župunski MD · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/nikolic2014-novi-sad-market-vegetables-metals">Comparative assessment of mineral elements and heavy metals accumulation in vegetable species</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food and Feed Research 41(2): 115-123, 2014</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/nikolic2014-novi-sad-market-vegetables-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>

<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing parsnips for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>

<a id="what-can-a-certificate-of-analysis-or-non-detect-establish-and-what-remains-untested"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 5 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 200 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Reporting limits for cadmium ranged from 0.5 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 20 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

A useful lot result names the form, the basis and a quantification limit well below the limit that applies.

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-parsnips-is-inorganic" data-anchor="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-parsnips-is-inorganic" data-question="How much of the arsenic in parsnips is inorganic?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How much of the arsenic in parsnips is inorganic?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Inorganic arsenic, the more toxic form, was measured in 3 results from 2 sources, far fewer than total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Dietary exposure to inorganic arsenic in the European population</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">EFSA Journal 2014;12(3):3597</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) 2014 reported inorganic arsenic at 9.2 µg/kg (mean) in parsnips, as sold from European dietary-survey occurrence data.<sup class="cite">[[sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Dietary exposure to inorganic arsenic in the European population</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">EFSA Journal 2014;12(3):3597</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
